Shewing that the work that has of late been going on

HE Error of thoſe who have had ill thoughts 

of the great religious operation on the minds of 
men, that has been of late in New-England, ſeems 
fundamentally to lie in three things; Firſt, In judging 
of this work a priori. Secondly, In not taking the 
Holy Scriptures as a whole rule whereby to judge of 
ſuch operations. Thirdly, . In not juſtly diſtinguithing 
the good from the bad. 
5 Fo -& NE greatly erred in the way in which they 
have gone about to try this work, whether it be a work 
of the Spirit of Gop, or no, viz. in judging of it a 
friori from the way that it began, the inſtruments 
that havebeen employed, and the methods that have been 
taken in carrying it on. Whereas, if we duly conſider, 
ſuch a work is not to be judged of @ priori, but à bos

Another effect of ſpiritual Pride is a certain ſelf

8. Another effect of ſpiritual Pride is Aſſuming : It 
makes it natur l to perſons lo to act and ſpeak, as tho' 


it in a ſpecial manner belonged to them to be taken 
notice of, and regarded, It is very natural to a per- 


fon that is much under the influence of it, to take all 
the reſpect that is paid him: If others ſhew a dilpo- 
ſition to yield him the deference of a pieceptor, he is 
open to it, and freely admits it; yea, it is natural for 
him to expect ſuch treatment, and to take much notice 
of it if he fails of it, and to have an ill opmion of 
others that do not pay him that which he looks upon 
as his prerogative, He is apt to clothe himſelf with a 
judicial and dogmatical air in converſation, and to take 
it upon him, and what belongs to him, to give forth 
his lentence, and to determine and decide. Whereas 


humility vaunteth not elf, and is apt to prefer others 
ex honour, One under the influence of ſpiritual Pride, 


naturally puts on the airs of a Maſter : Whereas one 
that is humble naturally has on the air of a Diſciple ; 
his voice is, © What ſhall I do? What ſhall 1 do, 
that I may live more to Gop's honour ? What ſhall 
„do with this wicked heart? He is ready to re- 
ccive inſtruction from any body, agreeable to James i. 
19, * Wherefore, my beloved brethren, let every man 
pe ſwift to hear, ſlow to ſpeak.” The humble Chriſ- 
tian thinks he wants help from every body ; whereas 
he that is proud thinks every body wants his help. 
Chriſtian Humility intreats and beſeeches; ſpiritual 
Pride affeRs to command, and warn with authority.
